## ⚠ Major: overstated, disputed, or absent from a source read or searched in full (12)

- **c-m02-02** (claim) — not found: “No registered trial tests an 11β-HSD1 inhibitor for skin ageing, and AZD4017 has no FDA approval.”
  - cited: `ajjan-2022-azd4017-skin-pilot-rct` (fulltext), `nct03313297-gc-sheald` (webpage)
  - The provided sources do not confirm whether other registered trials exist for skin ageing or whether AZD4017 has FDA approval.
- **c-m02-04** (claim) — partial: “The guideline recommends taking a detailed drug history to exclude exogenous glucocorticoid exposure (oral, inhaled, topical, injected, skin-bleaching creams, herbal tonics, joint/nerve injections) before biochemical testing.”
  - `nieman-2008-cushings-diagnosis-guideline` (abstract only): “After excluding exogenous glucocorticoid use, we recommend testing for Cushing's syndrome in patients with multiple and progressive features compatible with the syndrome, particularly those with a high discriminatory value, and patients with adrenal incidentaloma.”
  - The abstract recommends excluding exogenous glucocorticoids prior to testing, but does not list the specific exposure routes or products.
- **c-m02-05** (claim) — partial: “The guideline recommends testing patients with multiple and progressive features (particularly discriminating ones) or features unusual for age.”
  - `nieman-2008-cushings-diagnosis-guideline` (abstract only): “After excluding exogenous glucocorticoid use, we recommend testing for Cushing's syndrome in patients with multiple and progressive features compatible with the syndrome, particularly those with a high discriminatory value, and patients with adrenal incidentaloma.”
  - The abstract supports testing patients with multiple and progressive features with high discriminatory value, but does not mention features unusual for age.
- **c-m02-11** (claim) — not found: “The labeling changes did not add skin as an indication.”
  - cited: `fda-mht-labeling-request-2025-11-10` (webpage), `fda-mht-labeling-approval-2026-02-12` (webpage)
  - The provided FDA sources do not mention skin or address whether it was added as an indication.
- **c-m02-18** (claim) — partial: “Primary adrenal insufficiency causes hyperpigmentation particularly of sun-exposed areas, skin creases, mucosa and scars.”
  - `hannah-shmouni-2026-are` (full text): “Whatever the cause, the skin sign is the same and is mechanistically elegant: loss of cortisol feedback drives POMC-derived ACTH and α-MSH, so melanocortin signaling rises and the patient develops hyperpigmentation — darkest in palmar creases, scars, and mucosa — with congenital adrenal hyperplasia adding androgen-driven hirsutism and acne.”
  - The source confirms hyperpigmentation of palmar creases, scars, and mucosa in primary adrenal insufficiency, but does not mention sun-exposed areas.
- **c-m02-19** (claim) — not found (whole document searched for this figure): “Androgen-secreting neoplasms account for about 0.2% of hyperandrogenic women, and over half are malignant.”
  - cited: `martin-2018-hirsutism-guideline` (abstract), `hannah-shmouni-2026-are` (fulltext)
  - Neither provided source contains the prevalence figure (about 0.2%) or the proportion of malignancy.
- **c-m04-07** (claim) — partial: “Palmitoyl pentapeptide-4 (pal-KTTKS, Matrixyl) improved photoaging measures in a 12-week randomized trial funded by the manufacturer.”
  - `robinson-2005-pal-kttks-rct` (abstract only): “Caucasian female subjects (n = 93, aged 35-55) participated in a 12-week, double-blind, placebo-controlled, split-face, left-right randomized clinical study assessing two topical products: moisturizer control product vs. the same moisturizer product containing 3 ppm pal-KTTKS. Pal-KTTKS was well tolerated by the skin and provided significant improvement vs. placebo control for reduction in wrinkl…”
  - The abstract confirms the 12-week randomized trial demonstrating significant improvement in fine lines/wrinkles in photoaged skin, but it does not specify manufacturer funding.
- **c-m05-17** (claim) — partial: “The single 2017 study from the developer's group is PTD-DBM's whole evidence, with no human trials published or registered as of 24 September 2026.”
  - `hannah-shmouni-2026-ppg-aug` (full text): “Clinical caveat. Mechanistically the most interesting investigational hair-loss compound on this list, but human efficacy data is thin — most use is via compounding pharmacies. Effective only combined with a Wnt-pathway modulator (typically valproic acid). Trial stage: preclinical only; no human RCTs.”
  - The source notes only preclinical research by the developer's group and no human RCTs, but it dates from August 2026 and does not explicitly check through 24 September 2026.
- **c-m07-03** (claim) — partial: “Topical GHK-Cu's only indexed RCT evaluated post-laser healing.”
  - `miller-2006-ghk-cu-laser-rct` (abstract only): “To evaluate the role of skin care products that contain a copper tripeptide complex, glycyl-l-histidyl-l-lysine-Cu(2+) (GHK-Cu), in treating carbon dioxide (CO(2)) laser-resurfaced skin.”
  - The trial evaluated topical GHK-Cu on laser-resurfaced skin, but the abstract does not confirm it is the only indexed RCT.
- **m4-p15** (block) — partial: “Both clinical trials of LL-37 were run/sponsored by the peptide's developer.”
  - `mahlapuu-2021-ll37-phase2b` (full text): “The study was financed by Promore Pharma AB, Solna, Sweden.”
  - The provided text confirms the developer sponsored the 2021 trial, but does not provide sponsorship details for the 2014 trial.
- **m5-p04** (block) — partial: “Diffuse hair shedding occurring a few months after childbirth or a rapid low-calorie diet represents telogen effluvium.”
  - `hannah-shmouni-2026-are` (full text): “Telogen effluvium can follow rapid loss — usually self-limited; reassure and reassess.”
  - The text notes that rapid weight loss or sudden physiologic stressors cause telogen effluvium, but does not specifically mention postpartum shedding.
- **m5-p04** (block) — partial: “Smooth bald patches (such as in the beard or spreading across the scalp) with or without nail pitting represent alopecia areata.”
  - `hannah-shmouni-2026-are` (full text): “APS-1 (APECED), caused by recessive AIRE variants, presents early with chronic mucocutaneous candidiasis, hypoparathyroidism, and primary adrenal insufficiency, alongside vitiligo, alopecia areata, and nail or enamel dystrophy.”
  - The text discusses alopecia areata and notes nail dystrophy alongside it in APS-1, but does not describe the smooth bald patch presentation in the beard or scalp.

## Supported (for the record)

- **c-m01-01** — “The skin acts as a peripheral neuro-immuno-endocrine organ composed of four immuno-endocrine units: epidermal, dermal, hypodermal, and adne…” ← `slominski-2025-skin-neuro-immuno-endocrinology`: “This skin neuro-immuno-endocrine system is compartmentalized through the formation of epidermal, dermal, hypodermal and adnexal regulatory units.” · “Cells within these skin units produce and respond to hormones and communicate via neighboring cells, sensory nerves, and the circulation.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Communication runs three ways: soluble factors between neighboring cells, rapid neural transmission, and release into the circulation that can reach distant organs.” · “The framework of the skin as a neuro-immuno-endocrine organ is mechanistic biology validated across cells, tissues, and species.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “This is not a fringe idea — the hypothesis that the skin is a self-contained neuroendocrine organ, articulated decades ago, has since been validated experimentally across cell types and species.”
- **c-m01-02** — “Skin cells express enzymes such as CYP11A1 to synthesize steroids from cholesterol and enzymes including 11β-HSD1, 5α-reductase, and aromat…” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Skin cells express CYP11A1, the rate-limiting enzyme that cleaves cholesterol to pregnenolone, and can build glucocorticoids, androgens, and estrogens locally — or activate precursors delivered from the blood.[4] Local …” · “Evidence for cutaneous steroidogenesis rests predominantly on cell, tissue, and biochemical/mechanistic ex vivo studies.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “much of the local-steroidogenesis literature is mechanistic (cell and ex-vivo); it explains biology, but is not a dosing rationale.”
- **c-m01-03** — “Human scalp hair follicles kept in organ culture respond to CRH by producing POMC products, secreting cortisol, and down-regulating their o…” ← `ito-2005-hair-follicle-hpa`: “Thus, even in the absence of endocrine, neural, or vascular systemic connections, normal human scalp hair follicles directly respond to CRH stimulation in a strikingly similar manner to what is seen in the classical HPA…”
- **c-m01-04** — “In mice, genetic deletion of glucocorticoid synthesis in keratinocytes leads to spontaneous skin inflammation and exacerbated experimental …” ← `phan-2021-keratinocyte-glucocorticoids`: “In line with 11β-hydroxylase deficiency in human inflammatory skin disorders, genetic in vivo Cyp11b1 ablation and long-term GC deficiency in keratinocytes primed the murine skin immune system resulting in spontaneous s…” · “Keratinocyte-synthesized glucocorticoids regulate local immune homeostasis and set the epithelial immune tone.” ← `phan-2021-keratinocyte-glucocorticoids`: “Our findings provide insights on how skin homeostasis and pathology are critically regulated by keratinocyte-derived GC, emphasizing the immunoregulatory potential of endogenous GC in the regulation of epithelial immune…” · “Deficient local glucocorticoid synthesis is associated with human inflammatory skin disorders.” ← `phan-2021-keratinocyte-glucocorticoids`: “These results confirm previous studies indicating that epidermal steroidogenesis is dysregulated in prevalent inflammatory skin disorders, such as AD and psoriasis ( 17 , 18 ).”
- **c-m01-05** — “The skin runs shortened thyroid-like (TRH, TSH, deiodinase-generated T3) and somatotropic (GH/IGF-1) loops.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Beyond the HPA axis, the skin runs truncated equivalents of other central axes. A cutaneous hypothalamic–pituitary–thyroid loop (local TRH and TSH, with deiodinases generating T3) modulates keratinocyte proliferation, t…” · “Evidence for the hair follicle GH/IGF-1 somatotropic loop comes from human follicles studied ex vivo.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “The human hair follicle runs functional peripheral equivalents of the central axes: it synthesizes cortisol and operates a local HPA-like loop,[11] and ex vivo it sustains a hypothalamic–pituitary–somatotropic equivalen…” · “Evidence for the skin's thyroid-like mechanism/deiodinases comes mainly from mouse models.” ← `mancino-2021-thyroid-epidermis`: “The role of the deiodinases in the regulation of skin homeostasis and pathophysiology has mainly been investigated using conditional, epidermal-specific mice models of D2 and D3 expression ( 38 , 62 – 64 ).”
- **c-m01-06** — “UVB converts 7-dehydrocholesterol to previtamin D3 in human skin.” ← `bikle-2014-vitamin-d`: “D3 (cholecalciferol) is produced from 7-dehydrocholesterol (7-DHC) through a two-step process in which the B ring is broken by UV light (spectrum 280–320 UVB) radiation from the sun, forming pre-D3 that isomerizes to D3…” · “Vitamin D3 is carried into the circulation and hydroxylated to 25(OH)D and then to calcitriol (1,25(OH)2D).” ← `bikle-2014-vitamin-d`: “Vitamin D, whether produced in the skin from 7-DHC or absorbed from the diet, must be activated first to 25OHD and then to its active form 1,25(OH)2D.”
- **c-m01-07** — “Reviews describe a skin contribution to circulating androgens and estrogens, especially in older people.” ← `zouboulis-2004-skin-endocrine-gland`: “Human skin produces, activates or inactivates metabolically numerous hormones which are probably important for skin functions but also for functions of the entire human organism, such as sex hormones, especially in aged…” · “The skin-to-body endocrine contribution of steroids is supported by biochemical data showing steroidogenic machinery in skin cells.” ← `slominski-2013-skin-steroidogenesis`: “Most recently it has been established that skin cells contain the entire biochemical apparatus necessary for production of glucocorticoids, androgens and estrogens either from precursors of systemic origin or, alternati…”
- **c-m01-08** — “Cortisol excess thins the dermis and causes striae and bruising.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Sustained glucocorticoid excess is visibly catabolic: it suppresses fibroblast collagen synthesis, thins the dermis and epidermis, delays barrier and wound repair, and produces the easy bruising, striae, and atrophy fam…” · “Hypothyroidism causes dry, coarse skin and myxoedema.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Hypothyroidism gives dry, coarse, cool skin and myxedema; hyperthyroidism gives warm, moist skin and, in Graves' disease, pretibial myxedema.” · “The Aesthetic & Regenerative Endocrinology guide treats the aesthetic exam as a systemic screen.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “CLINICAL PEARL — THE AESTHETIC EXAM IS A SYSTEMIC SCREEN — Sudden skin thinning and easy bruising suggest cortisol excess; coarse, dry, cool skin suggests hypothyroidism; new acanthosis nigricans flags insulin resistanc…” · “The guide lists suspected Cushing syndrome (violaceous striae, central obesity, proximal weakness, new hypertension or diabetes) and rapidl…” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “— Suspected Cushing syndrome (violaceous striae, central obesity, proximal weakness, new hypertension or diabetes). — Rapidly progressive virilization (deepening voice, clitoromegaly, rapid hirsutism) — possible androge…”
- **c-m01-09** — “The Hormone Cell Atlas analysed the expression of 379 hormone and receptor genes in about 14 million single cells and nuclei across 47 huma…” ← `fei-2026-hormone-cell-atlas`: “Drawing inspiration from the Human Cell Atlas, we analyzed the expression of 379 hormone and receptor genes in a transcriptomic dataset comprising 14 million single cells and nuclei across 47 human tissues. Using hormon…”
- **c-m01-10** — “The atlas's authors describe its predictions and scores as putative and hypothesis-generating rather than functional hormones and receptors…” ← `hormone-cell-atlas-portal`: “Important: these predictions are based on transcriptomic data and should be considered as putative and hypothesis-generating, rather than as functional hormones and receptors. The user takes responsibility for validatio…” · “Transcript levels alone often fail to predict protein levels.” ← `liu-2016-mrna-protein`: “We conclude that transcript levels by themselves are not sufficient to predict protein levels in many scenarios and to thus explain genotype-phenotype relationships and that high-quality data quantifying different level…” · “A transcriptomic map indicates cellular gene expression capacity rather than direct proof of hormone secretion or downstream effect.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “These are transcriptomic predictions — they flag the genes a cell expresses, not the quantity of processed, active hormone it secretes. Treat them as a map to confirm, not proof of secretion.”
- **c-m01-11** — “The guide infers from the atlas that the skin, built from every major cell lineage, is plausibly one of the body's largest distributed endo…” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Second, it follows that the skin — the body's largest organ, built from every major lineage (epithelial, mesenchymal, endothelial, adipocyte, immune, neural) in vast quantity — is plausibly one of the body's largest dis…”
- **c-m01-12** — “Topical and transdermal treatments act through native receptors present in the skin, with local steroid-metabolising enzymes modifying pote…” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Therapy: topical and transdermal routes exploit the skin's native receptors — retinoids (RAR), vitamin D analogs (VDR), estrogens (ER), glucocorticoids (GR) — but local steroid-metabolizing enzymes modify potency, so th…” · “Receptor presence identified via transcriptomic atlas predictions is a hypothesis-generating starting point requiring validation and does n…” ← `hormone-cell-atlas-portal`: “Important: these predictions are based on transcriptomic data and should be considered as putative and hypothesis-generating, rather than as functional hormones and receptors.”
- **c-m01-13** — “The guide's testing pearl states that serum 25-hydroxyvitamin D falls with sunscreen use regardless of oral intake.” ← `hannah-shmouni-2026-are`: “Testing: serum 25-OH vitamin D partly reflects cutaneous synthesis, so it falls with low UVB exposure and sunscreen use regardless of oral intake.” · “An independent systematic review found that sunscreen abrogates vitamin D synthesis under artificially generated UV radiation.” ← `neale-2019-sunscreen-vitamin-d`: “In the experimental studies sunscreen use considerably abrogated the vitamin D 3 or 25(OH)D production induced by exposure to artificially generated UVR.” · “Randomized controlled field trials of daily moderate-SPF sunscreen found no effect on 25(OH)D concentrations.” ← `neale-2019-sunscreen-vitamin-d`: “The randomized controlled field trials found no effect of daily sunscreen application, but the sunscreens used had moderate protection [sun protection factor SPF) ~16].” · “High-SPF sunscreens have not been evaluated in trials assessing vitamin D levels.” ← `neale-2019-sunscreen-vitamin-d`: “However, there have been no trials of the high-SPF sunscreens that are now widely recommended.”
- **c-m01-14** — “Senescent cells are permanently growth-arrested yet continue to secrete factors.” ← `wang-dreesen-2018-senescence-biomarkers`: “Senescent cells are characterized by their inability to proliferate, resistance to apoptosis, and secretion of factors that promote inflammation and tissue deterioration ( Campisi and d’Adda di Fagagna, 2007 ; Kuilman e…” · “The senescence-associated secretory phenotype comprises components including IL-6, IL-8, matrix metalloproteinases, and growth factors, and…” ← `wang-dreesen-2018-senescence-biomarkers`: “For instance, senescent cells modulate their environment by secreting inflammatory cytokines, chemokines, matrix metalloproteinases (MMPs) and growth factors, collectively known as the senescence-associated secretory ph…”
- **c-m01-15** — “In human skin, cells carrying the senescence marker p16INK4a are more numerous with age in both the epidermis and the dermis.” ← `ressler-2006-p16-human-skin`: “The number of p16INK4A positive cells was found to be increased in both epidermis and dermis, compartments with strictly different proliferative activities.” · “No single universal marker unequivocally identifies senescent cells, making detection and quantification marker-dependent.” ← `wang-dreesen-2018-senescence-biomarkers`: “However, a significant hurdle in the aging field has been the identification of a universal biomarker that facilitates the unequivocal detection and quantification of senescent cell types in vitro and in vivo .”
- **c-m01-16** — “In 178 adults from the Leiden Longevity Study, higher numbers of p16-positive cells in sun-protected arm skin were associated with ageing-t…” ← `waaijer-2016-p16-wrinkles`: “The numbers of epidermal and dermal p16INK4a positive cells were significantly associated with age-associated elastic fiber morphologic characteristics, such as longer and a greater number of elastic fibers. Severity: *blocking* = a verified passage contradicts the course; *major* = overstated (partial), panel-disputed, or not found in a source that was read in full (likely misattribution); *minor* = not found in an abstract, excerpt or landing page, no verbatim passage, or no readable source text: a human with the full text decides.
